ParlaMint-AT_2022-02-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00143_d8e3781	I am very concerned about the independence of justice. Therefore, it was an essential step that I have taken to divide the great criminal law section in the Ministry of Justice, namely a section on criminal law and a section that is solely responsible for individual criminal matters. This means that, of course, the section responsible for criminal law has to engage in discussions in working groups, with delegates, with stakeholders, in order to design and write the laws. There is also a separate section that only works inwards and not outwards, and that is the section responsible for the prosecutors. It's very important to me that we go inside and not work outside. The second essential aspect, which contributes to ensuring the independence of justice, is, of course, its financial security. Resources in the field of justice are extremely important, so it was just a matter of increasing the budget for us Greens from the outset. We have done so in recent years, we have consistently increased the judicial budget. Not all the issues, not all the problems can be worked out and resolved from today to tomorrow, especially when one looks at the fact that justice has been under-financed chronically in the last ten years – unfortunately – that is what we have to say. That is why we have gradually obtained individual planning sites. At the prosecutor's office, we have achieved an increase of 10 percent, which is particularly pleased to have succeeded in me, especially when it comes to the independent investigation work of the prosecutor's office. But there are many, many other areas in which we have increased and will also increase.

The criminal procedure allows you to secure mobile phones, which you have also addressed. The subsequent evaluation of data stored on it is only possible if this appears necessary for reasons of proof. In doing so, both taxing and relief evidence must be collected, and all that is in the act is relevant to criminal law. That's what the prosecutor's office and what justice is doing with secure phones. This time, however, we also have the challenge of running alongside the Committee of Inquiry and that we are commissioned by the Constitutional Court and the Investigation Committee to provide the Examining Committee with information that has been provided to the Examining Committee, even after its abstract relevance, and to provide these data to the Examining Committee. The Information Order Act clearly determines how and in what classification these data are to be provided, and there are certain obligations as to how to deal with these data, including in the Investigation Committee.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-02-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00143_d8e6171	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Dear Austrians and people living in our country! We are currently faced with a situation that we all, together here in the House, have hoped, in fact, that it will never happen again, especially in Europe. At the same time, however, we have been confronted with the fact that war is back in Europe since the morning hours. Let me mention a particular aspect of a nation which is now at this moment, while we are talking here, trying with troops and by force to change its position! Russia, the Russian Federation, is a country of great history. It is above all a country that is closely linked to our history, if one goes far back in history; and it is above all a country that we also owe to that we have been freed from Nazi terrorism. Russian soldiers have fallen for Austrian democracy. At the same time, we are now experiencing that Russia is choosing a way that we deeply reject. The principle applies to us in Austria: It applies to the strength of the right and not to the right of the stronger. This is extremely important for Austria, because it means to us as a small country, and also as a country that has written neutrality in the main book that international law is essential to us. It is the basis of our history of time. International law is the foundation of the foundation of the Second Republic. We are facing a development that we have not considered possible. We actually have war again in Europe. What really impresses me deeply – and unfortunately in this case in a negative sense – is: Clearly, we are not able to learn from history, because European history is written with blood. In recent decades, peace has existed on the European continent, which has been like breathing up. Warning signs have always been enough: I am thinking of the 1990s and the decay of the former Yugoslavia. Even then we were surprised that conflicts can still be conducted with such brutality. Today, we are again experiencing politics linked to violence, although we in Europe in the various international organizations unisono have known that from the learning of history we are actually committed not to resolve conflicts with violence anymore, but to talk to each other, to put ourselves at a negotiating table – even if it is laborious, even if that means that one does not always get to 100 percent of what one wants – and in order not to abandon the price of peace for the sake of the negotiating table. One fact is that history, especially in Europe, shows: In war there are always only losers, and the first to lose are the weak in our society. The suffering is then always written up historically, there is a lot of scientific discourse about it, but the suffering always remains the same. The loss of people is always just as terrible, and in truth he cannot be justified by trying to enforce his political interests. It is indeed a serious situation that we are now experiencing in Ukraine. We experience a comprehensive invasion from the north, south and east. The great power of Russian Federation shows its entire military potential, from amphibious landings to airfielded divisions to the tank weapon to the far-reaching artillery. All of this doesn't sound so dramatic in words and sentences, but any artillery grenades, every shot can destroy a human life in the long run or actually damage it in the long run. There is, I believe, nothing that is just fatal and bad – even a learning from history. What do I mean by that? – It is this that the European Union has always been entrusted with all the discussions and problems we face at the head of government, at ministerial level, due to the 27-fold different interests that are being discussed and which are often passionately discussed – from economic interests to energy supply – since a long time ago. All of this is an intensive discourse in the European Union, but today and in the days before it is different: The European Union speaks with one voice. The European Union is committed to a Community approach, and this Community approach is so important for people in Ukraine at this time of need, in this incredible crisis and drama, and at the same time for us in the European Union. The European Union has made clear that it will set clear signs – translated: Penalties – when it comes to stopping those who now believe that they have to revise history, from the realization that war is the worst of all means to actually shape politics, because it is then designed with blood and misery. The Union has agreed to act clearly and in a spirit of sanctions against the Russian Federation. There is one aspect that has been discussed over and over again in advance. It's a small but not insignificant detail. Remember with me, the often asked question was: Is Nord Stream 2, the gas pipeline, because no subject of sanctions at all? Could it be that cynicism is so great that you simply let this project go through, even though so much injustice happens? The European Union has proved the opposite. In close coordination with the Federal Republic of Germany, Nord Stream 2 is on ice. This is just one of many measures that we will take to make clear to the Russian Federation that there will be no more understanding in Europe of this – we are in our history, that we owe our children – that policies and violence are combined. [[I should like to thank the rapporteurs of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection and the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection and the ……]] Especially in such a tense and indeed with regard to the information situation also diffuse time was the great concern: Is Austria going to be just as responsible as it is to act neutrally as a neutral country? That is why there has always been our participation in international organisations such as the European Union, the OSCE, but also our living solidarity within the European Union. For me, after two years of pandemic, the question now arises whether it is not excessive that the free tests are available at every corner, and whether they have also contributed to the low vaccination rate. For example, a patient said to me last week: Doctor, what is the vaccine for? I tested myself every two to three days. Then I said: Young man, this is a mistake, you're on the wood road. This wrong assumption – test is equal to replacement for vaccination – some are sitting on, as well as my patient. I've been talking to him for a long time. ParlaMint-AT_2022-02-24-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00143_d8e10989	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! Please allow me to begin by saying that I too refer to Ukraine. I belong to the post-war generation and have grown up all my life in apparently natural peace. At the same time, however, I also belong to that generation for which World War II and the First World War are not only chapters in the history book, but still vivid stories of my parents and grandparents about what these atrocities and these horrors have meant. I am in mind with people in Ukraine, and they deserve our whole solidarity. [[I should like to thank the rapporteur, Mr President, for his excellent report and for his excellent report.]] I would now like to address the request concerning complementary medicine, which is very broad. It calls for the establishment of professorships for complementary medicine, it also demands that they be provided in regular education, that they be integrated into the regular operations of hospitals and established doctors and are also financed. – Well, complementary medicine is a wide range of very different methods that have very little in common. If you ask yourself: Why do they run under complementary medicine, why are they not simply part of our medicine, which we always use and teach?, then is actually the binding element that there is no evidence of its effect for these methods so far. This is, of course, a point to deal with. It is now true that only the fact that a medical method is widely used and that there is also much approval is no substitute for proof of effect. This has often been demonstrated by medical history – and it has essentially two reasons why this assessment of effectiveness is so difficult. One thing we all know, it's in the mouth: the placebo effect. But there is something second, which is actually even more important: This is the self-healing power of nature. A lot of things are going to be all right or better by themselves. There we doctors are all called to more modesty. Not always, when something gets better, it was absolutely because of the action we have set. I mention a placardial example that we all have in mind: At more than 80 percent, a covid infection is somewhat bright. Let's assume we treat all the infected with something completely ineffective: 80 percent will report a good experience. We are on the point of view: What is really effective is part of the general business and general education. What is ineffective does not belong to it. I would like to draw particular attention to the subject of education and training: If a method is effective, it can actually become ineffective through poor training and poor practice – but if something is ineffective, it will not be effective by the best training. The application mentions the term holistic several times. Holisticity is something that medicine really needs. But this has nothing to do with complementary medicine, but holisticity corresponds to the so-called biopsychosocial understanding. This means that medicine is only in part bio/natural science, but to an equally important extent the psychosocial dimension has to be taken into account. My medical generation has often had to acquire this second experience very painfully after studying. The Austrian universities have been working intensively, at the beginning of the 2000s at the latest, to teach our young people already in their studies; and my home university, the Med-Uni Graz, which is not by chance one of the top 20 young universities worldwide, has made this psychosocial understanding their mission. I would like to expressly thank all my colleagues who give their patients this really comprehensive medicine – effective measures associated with the psychosocial dimension – and all teachers at the universities who teach it to our young people. - Thank you.

What vaccines have we donated and in what framework? – We donated 1 million doses of Johnson & Johnson vaccine at a time when the demand for Johnson & Johnson in Austria was no longer in place at the beginning of November. We have donated half a million doses to Astra-Zeneca vaccines in the main Bosnia-Herzegovina, and to Iran we have also donated Astra-Zeneca vaccine in several tranches. That is, we have donated vaccine that has not been used in Austria anymore. It must be clear to everyone that the pandemic is over only when it's over everywhere. In addition, we have ordered 5 million pieces of vaccination equipment because we know that in some countries – especially in Africa – where the vaccination rate is only 10 percent, there is no way of vaccinating people with pure ampoules. They also need pistons and needles. A total of about 1.5 million doses have been donated via the international distribution mechanism Covax, 3.3 million doses bilaterally. At the end of the year, the European Commission set up Hera, an EU crisis management and response authority. What's Hera doing? – In the event of a health emergency, a joint purchase shall be made: Vaccines, agents, therapeutics. We are benefiting from the fact that there is the EU because we are getting vaccines to the extent that we need them. We are just getting medicines to which there has also been a European effort. That is to say, there is also a concept of solidarity that we ourselves benefit from. One more step on this: The WHO pursues the One-Health approach. This is about the health of people related to animal health, but also to the environment. Consider the antibiotic resistance: It is, I believe, whether an antibiotic resistance arises in Africa, Asia or America: If an antibiotic no longer works, it also has no effect in itself. That is, the EU is following very consistently, also with the support of the World Health Organisation, the approach of the transfer of knowledge and technology to the global south. I warn against the idea that in April, after Omikron is over, the search took place – an ugly expression – that many people would have Omikron, we are going into an endemic phase. That's not what I said. We need a high rate of vaccination. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and the Deputies of Disoski and Prammer.]] Ladies and gentlemen, the term Hospiz, coming from Latin, means something like a hostel or a resting place. Palliative, also coming from Latin, means something like a coat. I think these are both very nice symbols: a coat you put around someone who needs him who needs care. Surprisingly, only in 1961 in a suburb of London was the first clinic for mortals and their families established, in which the first time also accents were set in the medical and psychological field. A holistic approach was adopted there, including family members. This was also the beginning of medical treatment for people at the end of their life and the beginning of pain therapy. Today, the World Health Organization says everyone has a right to pain. This is not always possible, but to a large extent. I think that was a very, very important step, and the taboo theme of death and death has thus come to the public's attention. Even through publications like those of Swiss physician Kübler-Ross, a lot has really happened, and the modern hospice movement has thus emerged in the sixties. With the establishment of the hospice idea in the health and care system, everything is done to ensure a decent and dignified life until the end. It is important that this applies both to the mobile services and to the daily hospice and all stationary facilities. I mean, everyone has his place and always the individual situation is crucial to what is the right way. Hospice care expresses so beautifully what it's all about: Not to leave people alone and to accompany people so that they can self-determined and uncomplained lead a conscious life. However, palliative and hospice work also needs very, very high standards, ladies and gentlemen, it needs regular helper conferences and supervision – we need to pay attention to that – and it also needs regular training. As I said, I think we're on the right track. I would like to stress that there has been a national association of hospice and palliative institutions in Carinthia – I am a Carinthia – since 2005 and in particular the Caritas, the Diacony and the Red Cross have pioneered. I would also like to stress that there have been very, very many organisations involved. Ladies and gentlemen, come to the conclusion: The last phase of life is always an exception for all concerned. There is a special suffering pressure, there are questions that have to do with overneath, with anger, with fear, with sorrow, with hopelessness, but also with the search for peace, and with farewell and mourning. This is an exceptional situation, and it is therefore so important to organize the right follow-up. ==References====External links== ==References====External links== If there's nothing more to do, there's a lot to do: Heal – sometimes, relax – often, comfort – always. This is also the guiding theme of the international Palliative Care movement. I think that's a very, very nice leitmotif. I would like to thank all those who today agree to this milestone for the hospice and palliative services in Austria. And once again a great thank you to all those who have contributed to the fact that we can make this decision today. Thank you.

The fact is that while pandemic development allows for opening steps, we also know that the disease for pregnant women is particularly dangerous. There is an exemption for pregnant women who are still valid until the end of March, and this scheme will be valid by three months – until 30.6. – extended and extended. So far, this scheme applies to pregnant women who have not been vaccinated. Now we know that vaccination in this omicron wave offers a very, very good protection against severe disease, but we still want to keep all risks as low as possible and thus extend the right of exemption even to vaccinated women in these near-body professions. That is, there is a claim for vaccines and non-vaccinations. Employers are fully reimbursed for the costs of the exemption. Nevertheless, I would like to point out, and we do so again and again, that vaccination also prevents serious history in pregnant women, that it is recommended for pregnant women, and that it offers the best possible protection against a disease with the corona virus. Thank you.

A Wifo study shows that disabled persons with disabilities have a lower risk of poverty than those who are not employed. There is the operating service for this purpose.
